\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma\times\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{2}}}J_{\epsilon}(y^{\prime}-y+(t^{\prime}-t)\xi)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y^{\prime}\xi}(t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2n}}{\epsilon^{n+1}}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}}J(y^{\prime}-y+(t^{\prime}-t)\xi)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y^{\prime}\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{n-1}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}J(y^{\prime}-y+(t^{\prime}-t)\xi)\,dt\bigg)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y^{\prime}\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{n-1}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}J(y^{\prime}-y+t\xi)\,dt\bigg)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y^{\prime}\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{n-1}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y-y^{\prime})|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y^{\prime}\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy.

For the second integral, we first notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}J_{\epsilon}(y^{\prime}-y+(t^{\prime}-t)\xi)\,dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{n}}\int_{\Sigma}J\bigg(\frac{y^{\prime}-y}{\epsilon}+\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{\epsilon}\xi\bigg)\,dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}\Sigma}J\bigg(y^{\prime}-\frac{y}{\epsilon}+\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{\epsilon}\xi\bigg)\,dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{V}J\bigg(y^{\prime}-\frac{y}{\epsilon}+\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{\epsilon}\xi\bigg)\,dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{V}J\bigg(y^{\prime}+\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{\epsilon}\xi\bigg)\,dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}J^{\xi}_{\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{\epsilon}\bigg)=J^{\xi}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t).

Therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma\times\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{2}}}J_{\epsilon}(y^{\prime}-y+(t^{\prime}-t)\xi)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{2}}}J^{\xi}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy,

where in the last equality we considered the change of variables given by the multiplication in the variables
(t,t^{\prime})
by
\epsilon
(the jacobian is exactly
\epsilon^{2}
).

Summarizing we have obatined

(6.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{A\times A}J_{\epsilon}(x^{\prime}-x)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x)|^{2}\,dx^{\prime}dx

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{d\epsilon^{n-1}}{d-1}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y^{\prime}\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{\epsilon y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+d\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy=:\frac{d\epsilon^{n-1}}{d-1}I_{\epsilon}+dI_{\epsilon}^{\prime}.

By taking into account
(X.2)
we continue the above inequalities as follows

\displaystyle I_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\epsilon^{1+\alpha}|y^{\prime}-y|^{1+\alpha}\,dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle+c\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)|\sigma_{\epsilon y^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\sigma_{\epsilon y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle=:cI_{\epsilon}^{1}+cI^{2}_{\epsilon},

for some constant
c>0
depending only on
A
,
\sup_{x\in A}|z_{1}(x)|
, and
\sup_{x\in A}|z_{2}(x)|
. Since
J
belongs to the class
\mathcal{L}_{n}(\eta,\lambda,\rho)
, the stability property proved in Lemma
5.3
tells us that
J^{\xi^{\bot}}\in\mathcal{L}_{n-1}^{\xi^{\bot}}(\eta,\lambda^{\prime},\rho/2)
for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,1)
. Hence, by applying Lemma
6.1
we find

(6.23)

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\epsilon^{n-1}I^{1}_{\epsilon}=0.

For the term
I^{2}_{\epsilon}
, we use (
6.19
) to infer

\displaystyle I^{2}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}|\sigma_{\epsilon y^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\sigma_{\epsilon y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\leq a_{\omega}^{2}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{(-R^{\prime},R^{\prime})}|\gamma_{\epsilon y^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})+a_{\omega}^{-1}b_{\omega}(\epsilon y^{\prime})-\gamma_{\epsilon y}(t^{\prime})-a_{\omega}^{-1}b_{\omega}(\epsilon y)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{\{-\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\leq t^{\prime}\leq-R^{\prime}\}}|z_{1}(\epsilon y)-z_{1}(\epsilon y^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{\{R^{\prime}\leq t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\}}|z_{2}(\epsilon y)-z_{2}(\epsilon y^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy.

From (
6.6
) and the regularity assumption on the wells
z_{1},z_{2}
we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{(-R^{\prime},R^{\prime})}|\gamma_{\epsilon y^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})+a_{\omega}^{-1}b_{\omega}(\epsilon y^{\prime})-\gamma_{\epsilon y}(t^{\prime})-a_{\omega}^{-1}b_{\omega}(\epsilon y)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\leq L^{\prime}\lfloor\partial_{t}W\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\epsilon^{\alpha^{2}}|y-y^{\prime}|^{\alpha^{2}}\,dy^{\prime}dy=:L^{\prime}I^{21}_{\epsilon}

where
L^{\prime}:=L^{\prime}(\delta,K,\|\sigma_{J^{\xi}}\|_{L^{1}},\|\overline{\sigma}_{J^{\xi}}\|_{L^{1}},\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
, and

\displaystyle\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{\{R^{\prime}\leq|t^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\}}|z_{1}(\epsilon y)-z_{1}(\epsilon y^{\prime})|^{2}+|z_{2}(\epsilon y)-z_{2}(\epsilon y^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}\epsilon^{2\alpha}|y-y^{\prime}|^{2\alpha}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy=:2I^{22}_{\epsilon}.

Since
\eta\leq\alpha^{2}
and
J\in\mathcal{L}_{n}(\eta,\lambda,\rho)
, we have

\int_{\xi^{\bot}}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y)|y|^{\alpha^{2}}\,dy\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}J(h)(|h|\vee|h|^{\alpha^{2}})\,dh<\infty,

which yields
\epsilon^{n-1}I^{21}_{\epsilon}\to 0
. In addition, since

\displaystyle\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}

\displaystyle J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})}\epsilon^{2\alpha}|y-y^{\prime}|^{2\alpha}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)dy^{\prime}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(\Sigma\times\Sigma)}J^{\xi^{\bot}}(y^{\prime}-y)\epsilon^{2\alpha-1}|y-y^{\prime}|^{2\alpha}\,dy^{\prime}dy,

we can apply again Lemma
6.1
with
\beta=2\alpha-1>0
to infer
\epsilon^{n-1}I^{22}_{\epsilon}\to 0
. Summarizing we obtained

(6.24)

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\epsilon^{n-1}I^{2}_{\epsilon}=0.

Now we consider the term
I^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
. We first notice that, under condition
(H.3)
, we can take the transition maps
\varphi_{\rho}
in such a way that
\varphi_{\rho}(x_{0},x,t)=\varphi_{1}(x_{0},\rho x,t)
. Thus,
(X.2)
entails

\displaystyle\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}

\displaystyle J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t\xi,\sigma_{y}(t))-\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t^{\prime}\xi,\sigma_{y}(t^{\prime}))|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)\epsilon^{2\alpha}|t-t^{\prime}|^{2\alpha}\,dt^{\prime}dt

(6.25)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +(1+O(\epsilon))a_{\omega}^{2}\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\gamma_{y}(t)-\gamma_{y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt,

where in the last inequality we used the fact that projections on convex sets (in this case truncation maps) are
1
-Lipschitz maps, and where
c
is a constant depending on the parameters involved in
(X.2)
. By using again the stability property of the class
\mathcal{L}_{n}(\eta,\lambda,\rho)
, we know that
J^{\xi}\in\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\langle\xi\rangle}(\sigma,\lambda^{\prime},\rho)
for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,1]
. We can thus make use of Lemma
6.1
with
\beta=2\alpha-1>0
in (
6.25
) to infer

(6.26)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle\leq a_{\omega}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\gamma_{y}(t)-\gamma_{y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt.

Thanks to the assumption
F_{0}(u)<\infty
, we have
\int_{\Sigma}\sigma(y,\xi)\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}<\infty
. Therefore, by (
6.25
) the sequence appearing on the left-hand side of (
6.26
) is dominated for
y\in\Sigma
as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
. We can thus make use of the limsup version of Fatou’s lemma to write

(6.27)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{2\epsilon})^{2}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}^{y\xi}(\epsilon t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle\leq a_{\omega}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\gamma_{y}(t)-\gamma_{y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy.

Eventually, (
6.22
), (
6.23
),(
6.24
), and (
6.27
), together with the fact that the parameter
d
in (
6.22
) can be chosen arbitrary close to
1
, allow us to deduce

(6.28)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\epsilon}\int_{A\times A}J_{\epsilon}(x^{\prime}-x)|u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x^{\prime})-u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x)|^{2}\,dx^{\prime}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a^{2}_{\omega}}{4}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\gamma_{y}(t)-\gamma_{y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy.

Step 4: Energy estimate for the potential
.
Now we examine the energy contribution due to the double-well potential. We first observe that by
(X.1)
and (
6.19
) there holds
\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(t))=z_{1}(y+\epsilon t\xi)
and
\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(t))=z_{2}(y+\epsilon t\xi)
for every
t\in(-\infty,-R^{\prime})
and
t\in(R^{\prime},\infty)
, respectively, for all
y\in\Sigma
.

Therefore,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{A}W(x,u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x))\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{A^{\xi}_{y}}W(y+t\xi,u_{\epsilon,\omega}(y+t\xi))\,dt\bigg)dy

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(y+\epsilon t\xi,u_{\epsilon,\omega}(y+\epsilon t\xi))\,dt\bigg)dy

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(-R^{\prime},R^{\prime})}W(y+\epsilon t\xi,\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(t)))\,dt\bigg)dy,

where in the last equality we used
(H.1)
.
By performing the change of variables induced by the increasing function
\sigma_{y}
(see Proposition
3.5
), we write

(6.32)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(-R^{\prime},R^{\prime})}W(y+\epsilon t\xi,\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(t)))\,dt\bigg)dy

(6.33)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y+\epsilon(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\xi,\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\xi,t))\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy.

In addition, for every
\epsilon<1
the integrand in (
6.33
) is dominated by

\sup_{y\in\Sigma}\sup_{x\in B_{R^{\prime}}(0)}\sup_{t\in(-M,M)}W(y+x,t)<\infty,

where
M>0
is such that
[z_{1}(y+x),z_{2}(y+x)]\subset[-M,M]
for every
y\in\Sigma
and
x\in B_{R^{\prime}}(0)
.
This together with (
6.20
) allow us to apply again the limsup version of Fatou’s lemma and write

\begin{split}&\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y+\epsilon(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\xi,\varphi_{1}(y,\epsilon(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\xi,t))\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy\\
&=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,\varphi_{1}(y,0,t))\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy,\\
&=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy,\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used
(X.3)
. Summarizing, we have obtained

(6.34)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{A}W(x,u_{\epsilon,\omega}(x))\,dx\leq\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy.

Putting together (
2.3
), (
6.28
), and (
6.34
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon,\omega};A)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a^{2}_{\omega}}{4}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t^{\prime}-t)|\gamma_{y}(t)-\gamma_{y}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

(6.35)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d(\dot{\sigma}_{\omega})_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy.

Next, we study the last term of the above inequality as
\omega\to 0^{+}
. By using that
(\sigma_{\omega})^{-1}_{y}(t)=\gamma^{-1}_{y}(\varphi_{\omega}(y,t))
for
t\in(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))
, an integration by parts in time shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d\dot{\sigma}_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy

\displaystyle=a_{\omega}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\varphi_{\omega}(y,(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y)))}W(y,\psi_{\omega}(y,t))\,d\dot{\gamma}_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy.

Therefore, by (
6.12
), (
6.16
), and the fact that
W\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\Sigma;\dot{\gamma}_{y}^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{n-1})
(here we are using the assumption
F_{0}(u)<\infty
), we apply Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, and we infer (recall that
a_{\omega}\searrow 1
and
b_{\omega}\to 0
uniformly in
y\in\Sigma
)

\displaystyle\lim_{\omega\to 0^{+}}\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d\dot{\sigma}_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{(z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y))}W(y,t)\,d\dot{\gamma}_{y}^{-1}(t)\bigg)dy

(6.36)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(y,\gamma_{y}(t))\,dt\bigg)dy.

Step 5: Construction of a recovery sequence near
\Sigma
.
In this last part of the proof we construct the desired sequence. Notice that, for every fixed
\omega>0
, by
(X.2)
, for
(y,t)\in\Sigma\times[-1,1]
we have

\begin{split}|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{1}(y+t\xi)|&=|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,z_{1}(y))|\\
&\leq\lfloor\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,\cdot)\rfloor_{C^{0,1}([z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y)])}|(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t)-z_{1}(y)|,\end{split}

and, analogously,

\begin{split}|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{2}(y+t\xi)|\leq\lfloor\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,\cdot)\rfloor_{C^{0,1}([z_{1}(y),z_{2}(y)])}|(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t)-z_{2}(y)|,\end{split}

Thanks to (
6.19
), for every fixed
\omega>0
, there holds
|(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t)-z_{1}(y)|\to 0
and
|(\sigma_{\omega})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t)-z_{2}(y)|\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
locally uniformly for
(y,t)\in\Sigma\times(0,1)
and
(y,t)\in\Sigma\times(-1,0)
, respectively. Now fix two compact sets
K^{+}\subset\Sigma\times(0,1)
and
K^{-}\subset\Sigma\times(-1,0)
, and consider a sequence
\omega_{n}\searrow 0
. For every fixed
n
we already know that

(6.37)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega_{n}})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{1}(y+t\xi)|\to 0,\ \ \text{ uniformly on $K^{-}$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}

(6.38)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega_{n}})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{2}(y+t\xi)|\to 0,\ \ \text{ uniformly on $K^{+}$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}.

In particular, by (
6.37
)–(
6.38
) there exists a sequence
\epsilon_{n}\searrow 0
such that

(6.39)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega_{n}})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{1}(y+t\xi)|\leq\frac{1}{n},\ \ \text{ for $(y,t)\in K^{-}$ and $0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{n}$}

(6.40)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega_{n}})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))-z_{2}(y+t\xi)|\leq\frac{1}{n},\ \ \text{ for $(y,t)\in K^{+}$ and $0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{n}$}.

Thus, defining
u_{\epsilon}(y+t\xi):=\varphi_{1}(y,t\xi,(\sigma_{\omega_{n}})_{y}(\epsilon^{-1}t))
for
\epsilon_{n+1}<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{n}
and every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then

(6.41)

\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\to z_{1}\ \ \text{ uniformly on $K^{-}$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}

(6.42)

\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\to z_{2}\ \ \text{ uniformly on $K^{+}$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}.

Eventually, by considering sequences of compact sets
K^{+}_{m}\nearrow\Sigma\times(0,1)
and
K^{-}_{m}\nearrow\Sigma\times(-1,0)
we can can exploit (
6.41
)–(
6.42
) and again a diagonal argument, to find a not relabelled subsequence
(u_{\epsilon})
such that

(6.43)

\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\to z_{1}\ \ \text{ locally uniformly on $\Sigma\times(-1,0)$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}

(6.44)

\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\to z_{2}\ \ \text{ locally uniformly on $\Sigma\times(0,1)$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}.

To conclude, we observe that, since
a_{\omega}\to 1
as
\omega\to 0^{+}
by construction, then (
6.35
) and (
6.36
) yield also (
6.4
).
∎

Our next result ensures that the surface tension
\sigma
introduced in (
2.7
) is an upper semi-continuous function.

Proposition 6.5
.

The function
\sigma
defined in (
2.7
) is upper semi-continuous.

Proof.

From Proposition
3.1
we know that there exists an increasing minimizer
\gamma_{0}
of (
2.7
) such that
\sigma(x_{0},\xi_{0})=F_{x_{0}}^{\xi_{0}}(\gamma_{0})
. Since we want to prove the upper semi-continuity of
\sigma
at
(x_{0},\xi_{0})
, we may assume with no loss of generality that
\sigma(x_{0},\xi_{0})<\infty
.

Consider sequences
\xi_{n}\to\xi_{0}
,
\rho_{n}\to 0
, and
(x_{n})\subset\overline{B}_{1}(0)
, and define
\gamma_{n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
as
\gamma_{n}(t):=\varphi_{\rho_{n}}(x_{0},x_{n},\gamma_{0}(t))
, where for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the function
\varphi_{\rho_{n}}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is the transition map given in
(X.1)
–
(X.4)
(in this case we have set
K:=\overline{B}_{1}(0)
). In particular, we immediately deduce
\gamma_{n}\in X_{z_{1}(x_{0}+\rho_{n}x_{n})}^{z_{2}(x_{0}+\rho_{n}x_{n})}
.

We first notice the pointwise convergence

(6.45)

|\gamma_{n}(t+h)-\gamma_{n}(t)|^{2}\to|\gamma_{0}(t+h)-\gamma_{0}(t)|^{2},\ \ \text{for $\mathcal{L}^{2}$-a.e. $(t,h)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}$},

and the uniform bound with respect to the
h
-variable of the following quantity

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\gamma_{n}(t+h)-\gamma_{n}(t)|^{2}}{|h|}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n}\lfloor\varphi_{\rho_{n}}(x_{0},x_{n},\cdot)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rfloor^{2}_{C^{0,1/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\gamma_{0}(t+h)-\gamma_{0}(t)|}{|h|}\,dt

(6.46)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n}\lfloor\varphi_{\rho_{n}}(x_{0},x_{n},\cdot)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rfloor^{2}_{C^{0,1/2}}|z_{1}(x_{0}),z_{2}(x_{0})|.

We further notice that, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}J(h)|h|\,dx<\infty
by assumption, then thanks to the continuity of the
L^{1}
-norm with respect to the action of the group of rotations of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we infer

(6.47)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|J^{\xi_{n}}(t)|t|-J^{\xi_{0}}(t)|t||\,dt=0.

We thus estimate

(6.48)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}|J^{\xi_{n}}(h)(\gamma_{n}(t+h)-\gamma_{n}(t))^{2}-J^{\xi_{0}}(h)(\gamma_{0}(t+h)-\gamma_{0}(t))^{2}|\,dtdh

(6.49)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}|J^{\xi_{n}}(h)|h|-J^{\xi_{0}}(h)|h||\frac{(\gamma_{n}(t+h)-\gamma_{n}(t))^{2}}{|h|}\,dtdh

(6.50)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}|J^{\xi_{0}}(h)|(\gamma_{n}(t+h)-\gamma_{n}(t))^{2}-(\gamma_{0}(t+h)-\gamma_{0}(t))^{2}|\,dtdh.

By combining (
6.46
)–(
6.47
) we immediately infer that the integral (
6.49
) goes to
0
as
m\to\infty
. By arguing as in (
6.46
), the integrand in (
6.50
) is dominated by the function
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
defined as

\displaystyle f(t,h):=\sup_{n}(\lfloor\varphi_{\rho_{n}}(x_{0},x_{n},\cdot)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rfloor^{2}_{C^{0,1/2}}+1)

\displaystyle\frac{|\gamma_{0}(t+h)-\gamma_{0}(t)|}{|h|}J^{\xi_{0}}(h)|h|.

Hence, we can apply Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem in combination with (
6.45
) to conclude that (
6.50
) goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
.

In addition, we exploit
(X.5)
and argue as in the proof of Proposition
3.13
to infer

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x_{n},\gamma_{n}(t))\,dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x_{0},\gamma_{0}(t))\,dt.

Therefore we have proved the convergence

(6.51)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}F^{\xi_{n}}_{0,x_{n}}(\gamma_{n})=F^{\xi_{0}}_{0,x_{0}}(\gamma_{0}).

But this means that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\sigma(x_{n},\xi_{n})\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}F^{\xi_{n}}_{0,x_{n}}(\gamma_{n})=F_{0,x_{0}}^{\xi_{0}}(\gamma_{0})=\sigma(x_{0},\xi_{0}).

The arbitrariness of the sequences
(x_{n}),(\xi_{n}),(\rho_{n})
allows us to infer that
\sigma
is upper semi-continuous at
(x_{0},\xi_{0})
.
∎

We are now in position to conclude the proof of the
\Gamma
-limsup upper-bound.

Theorem 6.6

(Recovery sequence)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be open, let
J\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to(0,\infty)
be an even function, and let
W\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
satisfy
(H.1)
–
(H.4)
. Let further
\alpha\in(1/2,1]
be given by
(H.1)
. Assume that either one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(1)

Q_{x}(t):=\partial_{t}W(x,t)+t
has a continuous inverse for every
x\in\Omega
and
t\in[z_{1}(x),z_{2}(x)]
, and
J
satisfies
2.9
with
\|J\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}=1

(2)

J\in\mathcal{L}_{m}(\eta,\lambda,\rho)
for some
\eta\in(0,\alpha^{2})
and
\lambda,\rho\in(0,1)
.

Then, for every
u\in CP(\Omega;\mathbf{z}(x))
there exists a sequence
(u_{\epsilon})\subset L^{1}(\Omega)
such that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
and

(6.52)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\leq F_{0}(u).

Proof.

Since
u\in CP(\Omega;\mathbf{z}(x))
we know that there exists a set
E
of finite perimeter in
\Omega
such that
u=z_{1}
on
E
and
u=z_{2}
on
\Omega\setminus E
. By the approximation result in
[
21
, Theorem 1.24]
we find a sequence
(E_{k})
of polyhedral sets, such that

(6.53)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{E_{k}}\to\mathbbm{1}_{E},\ \ \text{ in $L^{1}$ \ as $k\to\infty$},

(6.54)

\displaystyle|D\mathbbm{1}_{E_{k}}|(\Omega)\to|D\mathbbm{1}_{E}|(\Omega),\ \ \text{ as $k\to\infty$}.

Define
v_{k}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
as
v_{k}(x):=z_{1}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{E_{k}}(x)+z_{2}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega\setminus E_{k}}(x)
. Then,
v_{k}\in CP(\Omega;\mathbf{z}(x))
is a polyhedral function for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus, owing to Proposition
6.4
we find a sequence
(v_{k,\epsilon})_{\epsilon}\subset L^{1}(\Omega)
such that

(6.55)

\displaystyle v_{k,\epsilon}\to v_{k},\ \ \text{ in $L^{1}(\Omega)$, \ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$\ for every $k\in\mathbb{N}$},

(6.56)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(v_{k,\epsilon};\Omega)\leq F_{0}(v_{k}),\ \ \text{ for every $k\in\mathbb{N}$}.

Hence, thanks to a diagonal argument, to conclude the proof of the theorem, it remains only to show that
\limsup_{k\to\infty}F_{0}(v_{k})\leq F_{0}(u)
. To this purpose, we notice that for every
u\in CP(\Omega;\mathbf{z}(x))
we have
F_{0}(u)=\tilde{F}_{0}(E)
where

\tilde{F}_{0}(E):=\int_{\Omega}\sigma\left(x,\frac{D\mathbbm{1}_{E}}{|D\mathbbm{1}_{E}|}(x)\right)\,d|D\mathbbm{1}_{E}|(x),\ \ \text{ for $E:=\{u=z_{1}\}$}.

As a result, it is enough to show that
\limsup_{k\to\infty}\tilde{F}_{0}(E_{k})\leq\tilde{F}_{0}(E)
. In view of (
6.53
)–(
6.54
) and the upper semi-continuity of
\sigma
given by Proposition
6.5
, this latter inequality follows from a result due to Reshetnyak (see
[
27
, Appendix]
).
∎

We conclude this section with the proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

By symmetrizing we may assume with no loss of generality that
-A=A
. Given two sets
B,B^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
we denote by
B+B^{\prime}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\ |\ x=z+z^{\prime},\ z\in B,\ z^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}\}
. By changing variables we have

\displaystyle\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(A\times A)}J(x^{\prime}-x)\epsilon^{\beta}|x-x^{\prime}|^{1+\beta}\,dx^{\prime}dx\leq\int_{\epsilon^{-1}A\times\epsilon^{-1}(A+A)}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dxdh.

Now,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{m}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}A\times\epsilon^{-1}(A+A)}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dxdh\leq\mathcal{H}^{m}(A)\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(A+A)\cap\{|h|\leq N\}}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dh

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\mathcal{H}^{m}(A)\int_{\epsilon^{-1}(A+A)\cap\{|h|>N\}}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dh\leq\mathcal{H}^{m}(A)\int_{\{|h|\leq N\}}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dh

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\mathcal{H}^{m}(A)\text{diam}(A+A)^{\beta}\int_{\{|h|>N\}}J(h)|h|\,dh,

where we used that, from the simmetry assumption on
A
, we have
A+A\subset\overline{B}_{\text{diam}(A+A)}(0)
. Since
J\in\mathcal{L}(\sigma,\lambda,\rho)
we have
\int_{\{|h|\leq N\}}J(h)|h|^{1+\beta}<\infty
and
\int_{\{|h|>N\}}J(h)|h|<\infty
. Taking the limsup on both sides of the above chain of inequalities, we conclude that

\begin{split}\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\,&\epsilon^{m}\int_{\epsilon^{-1}A\times\epsilon^{-1}(A+A)}J(h)\epsilon^{\beta}|h|^{1+\beta}\,dxdh\\
&\leq\mathcal{H}^{m}(A)\text{diam}(A+A)^{\beta}\int_{\{|h|>N\}}J(h)|h|\,dh\to 0\text{ as $N\to\infty$}.\end{split}

Hence, (
6.1
) follows from the arbitrariness of
N>0
.
∎

7.
The
\Gamma
-liminf inequality

In this section we establish a lower bound for the asymptotic behavior of our sequence of functionals.
Along the whole section we fix
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and an orthonormal basis
\{\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{n-1}\}
of
\xi^{\bot}
. In the following we will make use of the notation
C_{\rho}(0)
,
Q_{\rho}(x_{0})
, and
V_{\xi}
, introduced in Definition
5.5
. We denote by
M
any integer such that (
5.1
) holds true.
In order to simplify the notation, we further set

S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}:=\{y+t\xi\ |\ y\in C_{\rho}(0),\ t\in(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)\}.

Given a function
u\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
and an
(n-1)
-dimensional cube
C_{\rho}(0)
oriented by
\xi
, we define
u^{yz}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
as

(7.1)

\begin{split}u^{yz}(t)&:=u(y+tz),\ \ \text{ for every }y\in\xi^{\bot},\ z\in V_{\xi},\end{split}

and we denote by
\overline{u}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
the
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extension of
u
, namely,

(7.2)

\overline{u}(y+t\xi)=u\left(y-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}m_{i}\xi_{i}+t\xi\right),\ \ \text{ for every $(y,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times\mathbb{R}$ and $m\in\rho\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}$}.

In the proof of the liminf inequality, we will often need to modify
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extensions on a scale
\epsilon
. We will argue according to the following construction.

Definition 7.1
.

Given a family
(u_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}
such that
u_{\epsilon}\colon S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define
\mathring{u}_{\epsilon,\xi}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
to be its
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic modification at scale
\epsilon
and in direction
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
as

(7.3)

\mathring{u}_{\epsilon,\xi}(x):=\begin{cases}\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(x),&\text{ if }x=y+t\xi\in S^{\xi}_{\rho}\\
\overline{z}_{1}(x_{0}+\frac{y}{t})&\text{ if }x=y+t\xi,\ (y,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times(-\infty,-\rho/2\epsilon)\\
\overline{z}_{2}(x_{0}+\frac{y}{t})&\text{ if }x=y+t\xi,\ (y,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times(\rho/2\epsilon,\infty)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}.\end{cases}

By construction
\mathring{u}_{\epsilon,\xi}
is
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic. For simplicity of notation, we have omitted the dependence of the above sequence from
\rho
.

We begin by stating a technical result, concerning a double liminf inequality. Its proof, relying on a directional blow-up argument, is postponed to Section
8
.

To a family
(\psi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon>0}
of
C^{1}
-diffeomorphisms of
\xi^{\bot}
we associate the kernels

J_{\psi_{\epsilon}}(y+t\xi):=J(\psi_{\epsilon}(y)+t\xi)|\det(\nabla\psi_{\epsilon}(y))|

for
(y,t)\in\xi\times\mathbb{R}
, and we define

(7.4)

F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{0}}(u;B):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times B}J_{\psi_{\epsilon}}(h)|u(x+h)-u(x)|^{2}\,dhdx+\int_{B}W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(x,u(x))\,dx,

for every
u\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
measurable and every
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
Borel measurable. Our double liminf inequality reads as follows.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
(\psi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon>0}
be a family of
C^{1}
-diffeomorphisms of
\xi^{\bot}
. Let
x_{0}\in\Omega
, and let
(u_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}
be a family of measurable functions
u_{\epsilon}\colon\Omega\to[-M,M]
. Assume that

(7.5)

u_{\epsilon}\to u,\ \ \text{ in $L^{1}(\Omega)$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}.

Then, by letting
v_{\epsilon}\colon S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}\to[-M,M]
be defined as
v_{\epsilon}(y+t\xi):=u_{\epsilon}(x_{0}+y+\epsilon t\xi)
, there exist
\rho_{x_{0}}>0
and a set of full measure
I_{x_{0}}\subset(0,\rho_{x_{0}})
such that, for every infinitesimal sequence
(\rho_{\ell})\subset I_{x_{0}}
there holds

(7.6)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\rho_{\ell}\to 0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{0}}(\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi};

\displaystyle S_{\rho_{\ell}}^{\epsilon})\rho_{\ell}^{1-n}\geq\inf_{\gamma\in X_{z_{1}(x_{0})}^{z_{2}(x_{0})}}F^{\xi}_{x_{0}}(\gamma)

(7.7)

\displaystyle-C\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\limsup_{\rho_{\ell}\to 0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\partial Q^{-}_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{1}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

(7.8)

\displaystyle-C\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\limsup_{\rho_{\ell}\to 0^{+}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\partial Q^{+}_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{2}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1},

where
F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{0}}
is the functional defined in (
7.4
), the maps
\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi}
are as in (
7.3
),
C:=C(M,Z,\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
, we set
\partial Q^{\pm}_{\rho}(x_{0}):=\partial Q_{\rho}(x_{0})\cap\{x\ |\ \pm(x-x_{0})\cdot\xi>0\}
, and
F^{\xi}_{x_{0}}
is given by (
2.5
).

Remark 7.3
.

It is worth noticing that Proposition
7.2
does not depend on the chosen completion
\{\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{n-1}\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
of
\{\xi\}
to an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. This is due to the fact that Lemma
8.11
, on which the proposition is based, enjoys the same property.

We are now in a position to state and prove the first main result of this section, namely the identification of a lower bound in the case in which the admissible kernels are integrable.

Theorem 7.4

(
\Gamma
-liminf with integrable kernel)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be open, let
J\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to(0,\infty)
be an even function fulfilling (
2.9
), and let
W\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
satisfy
(H.1)
–
(H.5)
. Then, for every sequence
(u_{\epsilon})\subset L^{1}(\Omega)
such that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, there holds

(7.9)

\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\geq F_{0}(u).

Proof.

We may assume with no loss of generality that
\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)<\infty
.

By virtue of the compactness result we know that
u\in CP(\Omega;\mathbf{z}(x))
and in particular that
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u})<\infty
.
Since projections on convex subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
are
1
-Lipschitz maps and since the constant
M
has been chosen satisfying (
5.1
), we deduce that
F_{\epsilon}(u^{M}_{\epsilon};\Omega)\leq F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)
for every
\epsilon>0
, where
u^{M}_{\epsilon}
is the truncation of
u_{\epsilon}
between
-M
and
M
. Moreover, property (
5.1
) together with
u(x)\in\{z_{1}(x),z_{2}(x)\}
for a.e.
x
, imply that also the truncated sequence satisfies
u^{M}_{\epsilon}\to u
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
. Therefore, without loss of generality, we assume that
u_{\epsilon}\colon\Omega\to[-M,M]
.

Now, let
x_{0}\in J_{u}
, and denote by
\nu_{u}(x_{0})
a unit vector orthogonal to the tangent space of
J_{u}
at
x_{0}
. Let
\{e_{1},\dotsc,e_{n-1},\nu_{u}(x_{0})\}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and let
Q_{\rho}(x_{0})
be the hypercube parallel to the given basis. By Proposition
4.2
, the
\epsilon_{\ell}
-traces of
u_{\epsilon_{\ell}}
relative to
\Omega
converge on
\partial Q_{\rho}(x_{0})
to
u
for a.e.
\rho
(sufficiently small depending on
x_{0}
), for every infinitesimal sequence
(\epsilon_{\ell})
. Therefore, by using that the
\epsilon_{\ell}
-traces convergence relative to
A^{\prime}
implies the
\epsilon_{\ell}
-traces convergence relative to
A
whenever
A\subset A^{\prime}
, and by the arbitrariness of
\epsilon_{\ell}
, we infer the following

(i)

for every
x_{0}\in J_{u}
the
\epsilon
-traces of
u_{\epsilon}
relative to
Q_{\rho}(x_{0})
converge on
\partial Q_{\rho}(x_{0})
to
u
for a.e.
\rho\in I_{x_{0}}
such that
Q_{\rho}(x_{0})\subset\Omega
,

where
I_{x_{0}}
denotes the set of
\rho>0
for which Proposition
7.2
is satisfied. In addition, since
x_{0}\in J_{u}
, there exists a subsequence
\rho_{\ell}\searrow 0
(depending on
x_{0}
) such that

(7.10)

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{1}{(t\rho_{\ell})^{n-1}}\int_{\partial Q^{+}_{t\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{2}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0,\ \ \text{ for a.e. $t\in(0,1)$}

(7.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{1}{(t\rho_{\ell})^{n-1}}\int_{\partial Q^{-}_{t\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{1}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0,\ \ \text{ for a.e. $t\in(0,1)$},

where we recall that
\partial Q^{\pm}_{\rho}(x_{0}):=\partial Q_{\rho}(x_{0})\cap\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ |\ \pm(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu_{u}(x_{0})>0\}
. By exploiting the fact that
(\rho_{\ell})
is a countable set, we can find
t\in(0,1)
such that, setting with abuse of notation
\rho_{\ell}:=t\rho_{\ell}
, the following condition holds:

(ii)

for every
x_{0}\in J_{u}
there exists a sequence
(\rho_{\ell})\subset I_{x_{0}}
with
\rho_{\ell}\searrow 0
such that the
\epsilon
-traces of
u_{\epsilon}
relative to
Q_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})
converge on
\partial Q_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})
to
u
and

(7.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{1}{(\rho_{\ell})^{n-1}}\int_{\partial Q^{+}_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{2}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0,\ \ \text{ for a.e. $t\in(0,1)$}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\frac{1}{(\rho_{\ell})^{n-1}}\int_{\partial Q^{-}_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{1}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0,\ \ \text{ for a.e. $t\in(0,1)$},

for every
\ell
such that
Q_{\rho_{\ell}}(x_{0})\subset\Omega
.

Eventually, by Proposition
7.2
, properties (
7.12
)–(
7.13
) entail:

(iii)

let
x_{0}\in J_{u}
; then the subsequence
(\rho_{\ell})
associated to
x_{0}
by condition (ii) also satisfies

(7.14)

\liminf_{\rho_{\ell}\to 0}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{0}}(\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{0}};S_{\rho_{\ell}}^{\epsilon})\rho_{\ell}^{1-n}\geq\sigma(x_{0},\xi_{0}),

where
\xi_{0}=\nu_{u}(x_{0})
,
\psi_{\epsilon}(y)=\epsilon^{-1}y
,
S_{\rho_{\ell}}^{\epsilon}=C_{\rho_{\ell}}\times(-\frac{\rho_{\ell}}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho_{\ell}}{2\epsilon})
where
C_{\rho_{\ell}}
is any
(n-1)
-dimensional cube of size
\rho_{\ell}
centered at
0
and oriented by
\xi_{0}
,
v_{\epsilon}(y+t\xi_{0}):=u_{\epsilon}(x_{\ell}+y+\epsilon t\xi_{0})
for
y\in\xi_{0}^{\bot}
, and
\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{0}}
is given by (
7.3
).

In view of (ii) and (iii), given
\tau>0
, the fact that for every
x_{0}\in J_{u}
the corresponding sequence
(\rho_{\ell})
is infinitesimal allows us to make use of the Morse covering theorem
[
18
]
and to infer the existence of finitely many points
(x_{m})\subset J_{u}
and strictly positive radii
(\rho_{m})
such that (we assume
u^{+}(x_{m})=z_{2}(x_{m})
and
u^{-}(x_{m})=z_{1}(x_{m})
but nothing would have changed with the opposite choice, since
\sigma(x,\xi)=\sigma(x,-\xi)
):

(7.15)

the
\epsilon
-traces of
u_{\epsilon}
relative to
Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
converge on
\partial Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
to
u

(7.16)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \int_{\partial Q^{+}_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})}|u(x)-z_{2}(x_{m})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\tau\rho_{m}^{n-1}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \int_{\partial Q^{-}_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})}|u(x)-z_{1}(x_{m})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\tau\rho_{m}^{n-1}

(7.18)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{m}\sigma(x_{m},\xi_{m})\rho_{m}^{n-1}\geq F_{0}(u)-\tau,

(7.19)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{\ell}\rho_{m}^{n-1}\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u})+\tau

(7.20)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{0}}(\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}};S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon})\geq\sigma(x_{m},\xi_{m})\rho_{m}^{n-1}-\rho_{m}^{n-1}\tau

where
\xi_{m}:=\nu_{u}(x_{m})
,
C_{\rho_{m}}
is oriented accordingly to
\xi_{m}
,
v_{\epsilon}(y+t\xi_{m}):=u_{\epsilon}(x_{m}+y+\epsilon t\xi_{m})
for
y\in\xi_{m}^{\bot}
(we omit the dependence of
v_{\epsilon}
from
m
in the notation).

We hence infer

\displaystyle\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\geq\sum_{m}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{m}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{4}\int_{S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon}\times S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon}}J_{\psi_{\epsilon}}(x-x^{\prime})|v_{\epsilon}(x)-v_{\epsilon}(x^{\prime})|^{2}\,dxdx^{\prime}+\int_{S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon}}W_{x_{m}}^{\epsilon}(x,v_{\epsilon}(x))\,dx

(7.21)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{m}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{m}}(\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}};S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon})

\displaystyle-\sum_{m}\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon})\times S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon}}J_{\psi_{\epsilon}}(x^{\prime}-x)|\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}}(x^{\prime})-\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}}(x)|^{2}\,dx^{\prime}dx.

Notice that, by performing the inverse change of variables considered in the above equality, we can rewrite the last term by means of the defect functional, cf. (
4.1
), as

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon})\times S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle J_{\psi_{\epsilon}}(x^{\prime}-x)|\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}}(x^{\prime})-\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}}(x)|^{2}\,dx^{\prime}dx

(7.22)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}),Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})),

where
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to[-M,M]
is defined as

\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}(x):=\begin{cases}\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(x)&\text{ if }x=t+t\xi_{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\ (y,t)\in\xi_{m}^{\bot}\times(-\frac{\rho_{m}}{2},\frac{\rho_{m}}{2})\\
\overline{z}_{1}(x_{0}+\epsilon\frac{y}{t})&\text{ if }x=y+t\xi_{m},\ (y,t)\in\xi_{m}^{\bot}\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho_{m}}{2})\\
\overline{z}_{2}(x_{0}+\epsilon\frac{y}{t})&\text{ if }x=y+t\xi_{m},\ (y,t)\in\xi_{m}^{\bot}\times(\frac{\rho_{m}}{2},\infty).\end{cases}

We claim that the defect functional in (
7.22
) goes to
0
as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
. To show this, fix
m
and consider the orthonormal basis
\{e_{1},\dotsc,e_{n-1},\xi_{m}\}
. Denote by
\Xi
the family of all coordinate
(n-1)
-planes
V
with respect to
\{e_{1},\dotsc,e_{n-1},\xi_{m}\}
. We split
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
as the disjoint union of two sets
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, defined as

\begin{split}A_{1}&:=\bigcup_{V\in\Xi}\pi_{V}^{-1}(\pi_{V}(Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})))\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\\
A_{2}&:=(\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))\setminus A_{1}.\end{split}

We further denote
\pi_{V}^{-1}(\pi_{V}(Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})))\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
by
A^{V}_{1}
. Let
V\in\Xi
, and suppose for example that
V\neq\xi_{m}^{\bot}
. We show that
\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};A^{V}_{1},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))\to 0
. To this purpose, by letting
e_{V}
denote the element of
\{e_{1},\dotsc,e_{n-1}\}
which is orthogonal to
V
, we further split the set
A^{V}_{1}
as the disjoint union of two sets
B^{V}_{1}
and
B^{V}_{2}
defined as

\begin{split}B^{V}_{1}&:=(Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})-\rho_{m}e_{V})\cup(Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})+\rho_{m}e_{V})\\
B^{V}_{2}&:=A^{V}_{1}\setminus B^{V}_{1}.\end{split}

In view of (
7.15
) we can make use of Proposition
4.4
in combination with the
C_{\rho_{m}}
-periodicity of
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}
to infer

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};B^{V}_{1},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cap(V+x_{m}+\rho_{m}e_{V}/2)}|u(x)-u(x-\rho_{m}e_{v})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle=\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\int_{\partial Q^{+}_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cap(V+x_{m}+\rho_{m}e_{V}/2)}|u(x)-u(x-\rho_{m}e_{v})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\int_{\partial Q^{-}_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cap(V+x_{m}+\rho_{m}e_{V}/2)}|u(x)-u(x-\rho_{m}e_{v})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Therefore, by adding and subtracting
z_{2}(x_{0})
and
z_{1}(x_{0})
in the above integrals, we can apply conditions (
7.16
) and (
7.17
) to infer

(7.23)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};B^{V}_{1},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))\leq 4\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\tau\rho_{m}^{n-1}.

In order to estimate the defect on
B^{V}_{2}
we first notice that

\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};B^{V}_{2},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))=\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cup B^{V}_{2}};A^{V}_{1},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})).

By setting

\Sigma:=[(V+x_{m}-\rho_{m}e_{V})\cap A_{1}^{V}]\cup[(V+x_{m}+\rho_{m}e_{V})\cap A_{1}^{V}]\color[rgb]{0,0,0},

we have by construction
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cup B^{V}_{2}}=0
in a neighborhood of
\Sigma
for every
\epsilon
. Hence, by Proposition
4.5
the
\epsilon
-traces of
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cup B^{V}_{2}}
relative to
A^{V}_{1}
and
Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
converge on
\Sigma
to
0
. Proposition
4.4
allows thus to infer

(7.24)

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};B^{V}_{2},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))=0.

The case
V=\xi_{m}^{\bot}
does not present further difficulties and can be treated analogously. In particular we obtain the validity of (
7.23
) and (
7.24
) also in this case. Therefore, we can finally infer

(7.25)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};A_{1},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))\leq c\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\tau\rho_{m}^{n-1},

for some dimensional constant
c>0
.

From elementary geometric considerations, if we denote by
\Sigma
the
(n-2)
-dimensional skeleton of
Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})
, we see that
\Sigma=\partial Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})\cap A_{2}
and that
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\Sigma)=0
. Therefore by virtue of Propositions
4.2
and
4.4
it is not difficult to argue similarly as above to deduce that

(7.26)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};A_{2},Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))=0.

By putting together (
7.25
)–(
7.26
) and recalling that
\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m})=A_{1}\cup A_{2}
, we eventually infer that for every
m
there holds

(7.27)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\tilde{u}_{\epsilon};\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}),Q_{\rho_{m}}(x_{m}))\leq c\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\tau\rho_{m}^{n-1},

for some dimensional constant
c>0
. We now insert condition (
7.27
) in (
7.21
) and by recalling (
7.18
)–(
7.20
) we continue the estimate as

\displaystyle\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{m}\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\psi_{\epsilon},x_{m}}(\mathring{v}_{\epsilon,\xi_{m}};S_{\rho_{m}}^{\epsilon})-c\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\tau\sum_{m}\rho_{m}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\geq F_{0}(u)-c\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u})\|\hat{J}\|_{L^{1}}\tau,

for some dimensional constant
c>0
. Eventually, the arbitrariness of
\tau
yields (
7.9
).
∎

We are now in a position to prove the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality for general kernels.

Theorem 7.5

(
\Gamma
-liminf with general kernel)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be open, let
J\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to(0,\infty)
be an even function satisfying
(K.2)
and let
W\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
satisfy
(H.1)
–
(H.5)
. Then, for every sequence
(u_{\epsilon})\subset L^{1}(\Omega)
such that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, we have

(7.28)

\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\geq F_{0}(u).

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
7.4
we may assume with no loss of generality that
u_{\epsilon}\colon\Omega\to[-M,M]
.

For
N\in\mathbb{N}
define the kernel
J^{N}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to[0,\infty)
as
J\wedge N
, and let
F^{N}_{\epsilon}
be the energy (
2.3
) defined by means of the kernel
J^{N}
. In addition, by letting
\sigma^{N}
be the surface tension (
2.7
) relative to the kernel
J^{N}
, we consider the functional
F_{0}^{N}
as in (
2.8
) with
\sigma
replaced by
\sigma^{N}
. Since
J^{N}
satisfies the hypothesis of theorem
7.4
, we already know that, if
(u_{\epsilon})\subset L^{1}(\Omega)
is such that
u_{\epsilon}\to u
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
then
\liminf_{\epsilon}F_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\geq\liminf_{\epsilon}F^{N}_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon};\Omega)\geq F^{N}_{0}(u)
. Therefore, if we show that

(7.29)

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)=\sigma(x,\xi),\ \ \text{for every $(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$}

we would infer the validity of the theorem by Beppo-Levi’s convergence theorem.

In order to show (
7.29
), recall that

\begin{split}\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)&=\inf_{\gamma\in X_{z_{1}(x)}^{z_{2}(x)}}\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma(t)-\gamma(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma(t))\,dt\\
&=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{N}(t)-\gamma^{N}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{N}(t))\,dt,\end{split}

for some
\gamma^{N}\in X_{z_{1}(x)}^{z_{2}(x)}
which is increasing and such that
\gamma^{N}(t)\geq(z_{1}(x)+z_{2}(x))/2
for a.e.
t\geq 0
while
\gamma^{N}(t)<(z_{1}(x)+z_{2}(x))/2
for a.e.
t<0
. Since we already know that
\gamma^{N}\in BV(\mathbb{R})
and
|D\gamma^{N}|=|z_{2}(x)-z_{1}(x)|
for every
N
, we deduce that there exists a subsequence
N_{m}
such that

\gamma^{N_{m}}(t)\to\gamma^{0}(t),\ \ \text{ for a.e. $t$}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ D\gamma^{N_{m}}\rightharpoonup D\gamma^{0},\ \ \text{ weakly* as measures}.

Furthermore, since
W(x,t)>0
for
t\in(z_{1}(x),z_{2}(x))
, by arguing as in Proposition
3.1
, we deduce that
\gamma^{0}
is an increasing function in
X_{z_{1}(x)}^{z_{2}(x)}
. By Fatou’s lemma we find

\begin{split}&\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{N}(t)-\gamma^{N}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{N}(t))\,dt\\
&\geq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N_{m}\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{N_{m}}(t)-\gamma^{N_{m}}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{N_{m}}(t))\,dt\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{0}(t)-\gamma^{0}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{0}(t))\,dt.\end{split}

On the other hand, since every
\gamma\in X_{z_{1}(x)}^{z_{2}(x)}
is admissible in the minimization problem for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we also have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma(t)-\gamma(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma(t))\,dt

\displaystyle=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma(t)-\gamma(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma(t))\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{N}(t)-\gamma^{N}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{N}(t))\,dt.

Hence, we have proved that

(7.30)

F^{\xi}_{x}(\gamma^{0})=\inf_{\gamma\in X_{z_{1}(x)}^{z_{2}(x)}}F^{\xi}_{x}(\gamma)=\sigma(x,\xi),\ \ \text{ for every $(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$}.

In addition, since
J^{N\xi}\leq J^{(N+1)\xi}
, then we have as well
\sigma^{N}\leq\sigma^{N+1}
for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular
\sup_{N}\sigma_{N}(x,\xi)=\lim_{N}\sigma_{N}(x,\xi)
for every
(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Moreover, being
\gamma^{0}
admissible in the definition of
\sigma^{N}
for every
N
, we also obtain

\displaystyle\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{N}(t)-\gamma^{N}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{N}(t))\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{N\xi}(t-t^{\prime})|\gamma^{0}(t)-\gamma^{0}(t^{\prime})|^{2}\,dtdt^{\prime}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}W(x,\gamma^{0}(t))\,dt\leq F^{\xi}_{x}(\gamma^{0}).

By combining the above estimates, we infer

\displaystyle F^{\xi}_{x}(\gamma^{0})\geq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)\geq F^{\xi}_{x}(\gamma^{0}),\ \ \text{ for $(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$},

which together with (
7.30
) yields

(7.31)

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sigma^{N}(x,\xi)=\sigma(x,\xi),\ \ \text{ for every $(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$}.

This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

8.
Directional blow-up of the energy at a point

Throughout this section we fix
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and an orthonormal basis
\{\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{n-1}\}
of
\xi^{\bot}
. We further assume that the kernel
J
satisfies (
2.9
), and we denote by
M
any integer satisfying (
5.1
).
For
\epsilon>0
we consider the functional
F_{\epsilon,x_{0}}
defined as

F_{\epsilon,x_{0}}(u;B):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times B}J(h)|u(x+h)-u(x)|^{2}\,dhdx+\int_{B}W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(x,u(x))\,dx,

for every Borel set
B\subset\Omega
and every measurable function
u\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
,
where

(8.1)

W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(y+s\xi,t):=W(x_{0}+y+\epsilon s\xi,t),\ \ \text{ for every }(y,s,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}.

8.1.
The family of non-local operators

We consider the one-dimensional kernel
J^{\xi}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
defined in (
2.4
) and we observe that we can directly verify from (
2.9
) that

(8.2)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(t)(1+|t|)\,dt<\infty.

We first construct a family of one-dimensional kernels depending on
z\in V_{\xi}
in the following way. Let
K^{z}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
be defined as

(8.3)

K^{z}(s):=J(sz)|s|^{n-1},\ \ \text{ for every }z\in V_{\xi},

and notice that

(8.4)

\int_{V_{\xi}}K^{z}(s)\,dz=J^{\xi}(s),\ \ \text{ for every }s\in\mathbb{R}.

Therefore, using (
8.2
) we have also

(8.5)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}K^{z}(s)(1+|s|)\,ds<\infty,\ \ \text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e. $z\in V_{\xi}$}.

We start with a preliminary result which tells us that the non local operator in the energy
F_{\epsilon,x_{0}}
can be decomposed by means of the one-dimensional and non-local operators associated to the kernels
(K^{z})
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
\rho>0
and let
u\colon\{y+t\xi\ |\ (y,t)\in C_{\rho}(0)\times\mathbb{R}\}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}}J(h)|u(x+h)-u(x)|^{2}\,dxdh<\infty,

and let
\overline{u}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
denote its
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extension.
Then the following identity holds true for every
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}}J(h)|u(x+h)-u(x)|^{2}\,dx\bigg)dh,

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(s)|\overline{u}^{yz}(t+s)-\overline{u}^{yz}(t)|^{2}\,dsdt\bigg)dy\bigg)dz.

Proof.

We apply the change of variables
h=sz
with
z\in V_{\xi}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\bigg(\int_{S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}}J(h)

\displaystyle|\overline{u}(x+h)-\overline{u}(x)|^{2}\,dx\bigg)dh

(8.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{\xi}\times\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\int_{S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}}|\overline{u}(x+sz)-\overline{u}(x)|^{2}\,dx\bigg)|s|^{n-1}J(sz)\,dzds.

Now fix
(z,s)\in V_{\xi}\times\mathbb{R}
. By writing
z=\overline{y}+\xi
with
\tilde{y}\in V
we can apply the change of variables induced by the map
\psi\colon\xi^{\bot}\times(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)\to\xi^{\bot}\times(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)
defined as
\psi(y,t):=y+tz
, and by observing that the determinant of its jacobian is constantly equal to
1
, we infer

\displaystyle\int_{S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}}|\overline{u}(x+sz)-\overline{u}(x)|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{-\rho/2\epsilon}^{\rho/2\epsilon}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)-t\overline{y}}|\overline{u}(y+tz+sz)-\overline{u}(y+tz)|^{2}\,dy\bigg)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-\rho/2\epsilon}^{\rho/2\epsilon}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}|\overline{u}(y+tz+sz)-\overline{u}(y+tz)|^{2}\,dy\bigg)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{-\rho/2\epsilon}^{\rho/2\epsilon}|\overline{u}^{yz}(t+s)-\overline{u}^{yz}(t)|^{2}\,dt\bigg)dy,

where in the last but one equality we used the
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodicity of
\overline{u}
. By recalling the definition of
K^{z}
(
8.3
) we can insert the last equality in (
8.6
) and conclude the proof.
∎

8.2.
The family of potentials

By letting
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}^{\rho\xi}
and
a_{x_{0}}(y,\rho),b_{x_{0}}(y,\rho)
be defined as in (
5.12
) and (
5.15
), respectively, we further simplify the notation by dropping the dependence on
x_{0}
, namely, we set

\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}:=\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}^{\rho\xi}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ a_{\rho}(y):=a_{x_{0}}(y,\rho)\ \ \text{ and }\ \ b_{\rho}(y):=b_{x_{0}}(y,\rho).

For every sufficiently small
\rho>0
and every measurable function
\gamma\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
we consider the one dimensional functional
\mathcal{F}^{\rho\xi}_{y}
defined as

(8.7)

\mathcal{F}^{\rho\xi}_{y}(\gamma):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}J^{\xi}(s)|\gamma(t+s)-\gamma(t)|^{2}\,dtds+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}(y,\gamma(t))\,dt,\ \ y\in\xi^{\bot}.

By applying Proposition
3.1
we recall that for every
y\in\xi^{\bot}
there exists an increasing function
\gamma_{y,\rho}\in X^{b_{\rho}(y)}_{a_{\rho}(y)}
which is a minimizer of (
8.7
). We are now in position to define the family of potentials.

Definition 8.2
.

For
z\in V_{\xi}
, let
H^{z}_{\rho}
be the operator given in (
3.11
) relative to the kernel
K^{z}
and wells
\{b_{\rho}(y),a_{\rho}(y)\}
. We define the potential
P^{z}_{\rho}\colon\xi^{\bot}\times\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
as

P^{z}_{\rho}(y,t):=\begin{cases}(H^{z}_{\rho}\gamma_{y,\rho}^{-1})(t),&\text{ if }t\in(a_{\rho}(y),b_{\rho}(y))\text{ and }y\in C_{\rho}(0)\\
0,&\text{ if }t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(a_{\rho}(y),b_{\rho}(y))\text{ and }y\in C_{\rho}(0),\end{cases}

and we complete its definition for
(y,t)\in(V\setminus C_{\rho}(0))\times\mathbb{R}
by taking its
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extension.

Remark 8.3
.

It is important to observe that, by virtue of our assumption
J>0
and by virtue of Proposition
3.7
, the function
P_{\rho}^{z}(y,\cdot)
is continuous and strictly positive on
(a_{\rho}(y),b_{\rho}(y))
.

Definition 8.4
.

For every measurable function
\gamma\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
consider the following non-local and one-dimensional operators

(8.8)

\displaystyle\textbf{K}^{z}(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}}K^{z}(s)|\gamma(t+s)-\gamma(t)|^{2}\,dsdt,

(8.9)

\displaystyle\textbf{K}^{z}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(s)|\gamma(t+s)-\gamma(t)|^{2}\,dsdt

.
For
(y,z)\in\xi^{\bot}\times V_{\xi}
we define the following one-dimensional functionals

\displaystyle G^{yz}_{\rho}(\gamma):=\textbf{K}^{z}(\gamma)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,\gamma(t))\,dt

\displaystyle G^{yz}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\gamma):=\textbf{K}^{z}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\gamma)+\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,\gamma(t))\,dt,

for every measurable function
\gamma\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.

The next proposition provides a lower-bound for the family of our original energies.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
u\colon\{y+t\xi\ |\ (y,t)\in C_{\rho}(0)\times\mathbb{R}\}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function and let
\overline{u}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
denote its
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extension. Then, for every
\rho>0
small enough (depending on
x_{0}
) and for every
\epsilon>0
, there holds

(8.10)

F_{\epsilon,x_{0}}(u;S_{\rho}^{\epsilon})\geq\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}G^{yz}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\overline{u}^{yz})\,dy\bigg)dz.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
8.1
, it remains to prove that

(8.11)

\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(x,u(x))\,dx\geq\int_{V_{\xi}\times C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,\overline{u}^{yz}(t))\,dt\bigg)dydz.

To this purpose, we notice that the operator
H_{\rho,\xi}:Y\to C_{0}([a_{\rho}(y),b_{\rho}(y)])^{+}
given in (
3.11
) relative to the kernel
J^{\xi}
and wells
\{b_{\rho}(y),a_{\rho}(y)\}
satisfies by (
8.4
) the equality

(8.12)

\int_{V_{\xi}}H^{z}_{\rho}\,dz=H_{\rho,\xi},\ \ \text{ for every $\rho>0$}.

Therefore, by Theorem
3.8
we infer

(8.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}(y,t)\geq\int_{V_{\xi}}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,t)\,dz,\ \ \text{ for }y\in C_{\rho}(0)\text{ and }t\in\mathbb{R}

(8.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}(y,t)=\int_{V_{\xi}}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,t)\,dz,\ \ \text{ for }y\in C_{\rho}(0)\text{ and }t\in\text{supp}\,\dot{\gamma}_{y,\rho}^{-1}.

Notice that, by the very definition of
W^{\epsilon}_{x_{0}}
(see (
8.1
)), for
y\in C_{\rho}(0)
,
s\in(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)
, and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, there holds

W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(y+s\xi,t)\geq\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}(y,t).

Therefore, by denoting
\pi_{\xi}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\xi^{\bot}
the orthogonal projection, (
8.13
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(x,u(x))\,dx=\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}W_{x_{0}}^{\epsilon}(y+t\xi,u(y+t\xi))\,dx\geq\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}\mathcal{W}^{\rho\xi}(y,u(y+t\xi))\,dydt

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\geq\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}\bigg(\int_{V_{\xi}}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,u(y+t\xi))\,dz\bigg)dtdy=\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{S^{\epsilon}_{\rho}}P^{z}_{\rho}(\pi_{\xi}(x),\overline{u}(x))\,dx\bigg)dz

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{(C_{\rho}(0)-t\pi_{\xi}(z))\times(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}P^{z}_{\rho}(\pi_{\xi}(y+tz),\overline{u}(y+tz))\,dydt\bigg)dz

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{V_{\xi}\times C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,\overline{u}^{yz}(t))\,dt\bigg)dydz,

where in the second-to-last equality we used that the Jacobian of the map
\psi\colon\xi^{\bot}\times(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)\to\xi^{\bot}\times(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)
defined as
\psi(y,t):=y+tz
has determinant constantly equal to
1
, whereas in the last equality we used the
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodicity of both
P^{z}_{\rho}
and
\overline{u}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Now we explicitly compute the value of the the integral on the right hand-side of (
8.10
) when computed on optimal profiles
\gamma_{\rho,y}
:

\displaystyle\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}G^{yz}_{\rho}(\gamma_{y,\rho})\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\mathbf{K}^{z}(\gamma_{y,\rho})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,\gamma_{y,\rho}(t))\,dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\mathbf{K}^{z}(\gamma_{y,\rho})+\int_{a_{\rho}(y)}^{b_{\rho}(y)}P^{z}_{\rho}(y,s)\,d\dot{\gamma}^{-1}_{y,\rho}(s)\bigg)dy,

hence, by taking the integral with respect to
z
and recalling (
8.12
) in combination with Theorem
3.8
, we finally have

(8.15)

\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}G^{yz}_{\rho}(\gamma_{y,\rho})\,dy\bigg)dz=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\mathcal{F}^{\rho\xi}_{y}(\gamma_{y,\rho})\,dy.

Remark 8.6
.

Consider
\psi\colon\xi^{\bot}\to\xi^{\bot}
to be any
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism. Then, by defining
J_{\psi}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to(0,\infty)
as
J(y+t\xi):=J(\psi(y)+t\xi)|\det(\nabla\psi(y))|
and the corresponding kernel
K^{z}_{\psi}
exactly as in (
8.3
), we have

(8.16)

\int_{V_{\xi}}K^{z}_{\psi}(s)\,dz=\int_{V_{\xi}}K^{z}(s)\,dz=J^{\xi}(s),\ \ \text{ for every }s\in\mathbb{R}.

Furthermore, if we denote by
(G^{yz}_{\rho,\psi})
the family constructed with the kernel
J_{\psi}
, thanks to (
8.12
), equality (
8.16
) shows that (
8.15
) still remains valid, namely,

(8.17)

\int_{V_{\xi}}\bigg(\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}G^{yz}_{\rho,\psi}(\gamma_{y,\rho})\,dy\bigg)dz=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\mathcal{F}^{y\xi}_{\rho}(\gamma_{y,\rho})\,dy.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

8.3.
The double liminf inequality

We begin by introducing suitable further modifications of
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic extensions depending on
z\in V_{\xi}
and
\epsilon>0
.

Definition 8.7
.

Given a family
(u_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}
such that
u_{\epsilon}\colon S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define
\hat{u}_{\epsilon,z}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\mathbb{R}
to be the
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic modification at scale
\epsilon
and in the direction
z\in V_{\xi}
as

(8.18)

\hat{u}_{\epsilon,z}(x):=\begin{cases}\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(x),&\text{ if }x=y+tz\in S_{\rho}^{\xi}\\
\overline{a}_{\rho}(y)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}&\text{ if }x=y+tz,\ (y,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times(-\infty,-\rho/2\epsilon)\\
\overline{b}_{\rho}(y)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}&\text{ if }x=y+tz,\ (y,t)\in\xi^{\bot}\times(\rho/2\epsilon,\infty)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}.\end{cases}

By construction
\hat{u}_{\epsilon,z}
is
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodic. Notice that we do not explicitly write in the notation the dependence of
\hat{u}_{\epsilon,z}
from
\rho
.

We recall the definition of oscillation of a function .

Definition 8.8

(Oscillation)
.

Given a measurable function
v\colon C_{\rho}(0)\to\mathbb{R}
we define the oscillation of
v
in
C_{\rho}(0)
as

(8.19)

\emph{osc}(v;C_{\rho}(0)):=\esup_{y\in C_{\rho}(0)}v(y)-\einf_{y\in C_{\rho}(0)}v(y).

The next result shows that the difference
\textbf{K}^{z}(\hat{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,z})-\textbf{K}^{z}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})
can be controlled asymptotically as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Let
x_{0}\in\Omega
, let
(u_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}
be a family of measurable functions
u_{\epsilon}\colon S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}\to[-M,M]
. Then, for every
\rho>0
small enough (depending on
x_{0}
), and for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}
, there holds

(8.20)

\begin{split}\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0+}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}|\textbf{\emph{K}}^{z}(\hat{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,z})-\textbf{\emph{K}}^{z}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})|\,dy&\leq C\rho^{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}}+\rho^{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|K^{z}\|_{L^{1}}),\end{split}

where
C:=C(M,Z,\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
.

Proof.

In order to simplify the notation we assume
x_{0}=0
. By using the identity
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a-b)(a+b)
(
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
) we obtain the following pointwise estimate for
y\in C_{\rho}(0)
:

\displaystyle|\textbf{K}^{z}(\hat{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,z})-\textbf{K}^{z}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})\times(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{a}_{\rho}(y)-\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+c\int_{(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)\times(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{b}_{\rho}(y)-\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+\int_{(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)\times(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+\int_{(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+c\int_{(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})\times(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)\,dtdt^{\prime}

where the constant
c
depends on
M
and
Z
(
\rho\ll 1
), and where
\pi_{\xi}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to\xi^{\bot}
denotes the orthogonal projection. Now we estimate separately for every
y\in C_{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle|\overline{b}_{\rho}(y)-\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|

\displaystyle\leq|\overline{b}_{\rho}(y)-\overline{b}_{\rho}(0)|+|\overline{z}_{2}(0)-\overline{b}_{\rho}(0)|

(8.21)

\displaystyle+|\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-\overline{z}_{2}(0)|

\displaystyle\leq\text{osc}(b_{\rho},C_{\rho}(0))+2\,\text{osc}(z_{2},Q_{\rho})

(8.22)

\displaystyle\leq 3\,\text{osc}(z_{2},Q_{\rho})\leq 3n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}}\rho^{\alpha}

and analogously for every
y\in C_{\rho}(0)

(8.23)

|\overline{a}_{\rho}(y)-\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|\leq 3n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}}\rho^{\alpha}.

Additionally, for every
z\in V_{\xi}
for which
\pi_{\xi}(z)\notin\{\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}m_{i}\xi_{i}/2\ |\ m\in\rho\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}\}
(hence for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}
) there exists a small enough threshold
0<\epsilon_{z}<1
and there exists
m\in\rho\mathbb{Z}^{n-1}
such that
y+\pi_{\xi}(z)
belongs to
C_{\rho}(0)+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}m_{i}\xi_{i}
whenever
|y|<\epsilon_{z}
(loosely speaking they belong to the same copy of
C_{\rho}(0)
obtained by translating of
\sum_{i}m_{i}\xi_{i}
). This means that, for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}
we find
0<\epsilon_{z}<1
such that for every
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{z}\rho/2
,
y\in C_{\rho}(0)
, and
t,t^{\prime}\in(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)
, we can estimate

\displaystyle|\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-\overline{z}_{2}(\frac{y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|

\displaystyle=|z_{2}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-z_{2}(\frac{y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|

(8.24)

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}}|y|^{\alpha}\bigg|\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{t^{\prime}}\bigg|^{\alpha}.

Analogously, for every
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{z}\rho/2
,
y\in C_{\rho}(0)
, and
t,t^{\prime}\in(\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\infty)
, we have

(8.25)

|\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t^{\prime}}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))|\leq\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}}|y|^{\alpha}\bigg|\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{t^{\prime}}\bigg|^{\alpha}.

By recalling the definition of the defect functional (
4.1
), we can thus write for every
y\in C_{\rho}(0)
, for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}
, and for every
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{z}\rho/2

\displaystyle|\textbf{K}^{z}(\hat{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,z})-\textbf{K}^{z}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\,\rho^{\alpha}\,\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})},(-\infty,-\rho/2),(-\rho/2,\rho/2))

\displaystyle+C^{\prime}\,\rho^{\alpha}\,\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})},(\rho/2,\infty),(-\rho/2,\rho/2))

\displaystyle+C^{\prime}\|K^{z}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\rho^{2\alpha}\int_{\{|t|\geq 1\}}\frac{dt}{t^{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle+C^{\prime}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2}))},(-\infty,-\rho/2),(\rho/2,\infty)),

where
C^{\prime}:=C^{\prime}(M,Z,\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
.
In particular, the above chain of inequalities tells us that the family of integrands in the left-hand side of (
8.20
) is uniformly dominated with respect to
y
for every
\epsilon
small enough (depending on
z\in V_{\xi}
). Moreover, from Proposition
4.4
we have

(8.26)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})},(\rho/2,\infty),(-\rho/2,\rho/2))\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}

(8.27)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})},(-\infty,-\rho/2),(-\rho/2,\rho/2))\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}

(8.28)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon}\Lambda_{\epsilon}(\mathbbm{1}_{(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2}))},(-\infty,-\rho/2),(\rho/2,\infty))=0,

where we have set
\Sigma=\{\frac{\rho}{2}\}
,
\Sigma=\{-\frac{\rho}{2}\}
, and
\Sigma=\{0\}
in (
8.26
), (
8.27
), and (
8.28
), respectively.

We are thus in position to apply the limsup version of Fatou’s lemma in the left-hand side of (
8.20
) and to infer the validity of the proposition.
∎

We further show that for sequences
(u_{\epsilon})
converging in
L^{1}
the difference
\textbf{K}^{z}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})-\textbf{K}^{z}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\mathring{u}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})
can be controlled asymptotically as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Proposition 8.10
.

Let
x_{0}\in\Omega
, let
(u_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}
be a family of measurable functions
u_{\epsilon}\colon\Omega\to[-M,M]
, and let
v_{\epsilon}\colon S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}\to[-M,M]
be defined as
v_{\epsilon}(y+t\xi):=u_{\epsilon}(x_{0}+y+\epsilon t\xi)
. Assume that

(8.29)

u_{\epsilon}\to u,\ \ \text{ in $L^{1}(\Omega)$ as $\epsilon\to 0^{+}$}.

Then, for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e. sufficiently small
\rho>0
(depending on
x_{0}
), and for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}
, there holds

(8.30)

\begin{split}\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0+}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}|\textbf{\emph{K}}^{z}(\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})-\textbf{\emph{K}}_{\rho,\epsilon}^{z}(\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})|\,dy&\leq C\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\partial Q^{-}_{\rho}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{1}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
&+C\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\partial Q^{+}_{\rho}(x_{0})}|u(x)-z_{2}(x_{0})|\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
&+C\rho^{2\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\|K^{z}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\hat{K}^{z}\|_{L^{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}),\end{split}

where
\partial Q^{\pm}_{\rho}(x_{0}):=\partial Q_{\rho}(x_{0})\cap\{x\ |\ \pm(x-x_{0})\cdot\xi>0\}
and
C:=C(M,Z,\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
.

Proof.

In order to simplify the notation, we assume that
x_{0}=0
and
\{y+t\xi\ |\ y\in C_{\rho}(0),\ t\in(-1/2,1/2)\}\subset\Omega
for every
\rho>0
small enough.

We notice that, from the very definition of
\mathring{v}_{\epsilon}
, we have for every
y\in C_{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle\textbf{K}^{z}(\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})-\textbf{K}^{z}_{\rho,\epsilon}(\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi})

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon}))}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t^{\prime})-\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt.

We split the integration domain as

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon))=(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon))^{2}

\displaystyle\cup(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)\times(-\infty,-\rho/2\epsilon,)

\displaystyle\cup(-\rho/2\epsilon,\rho/2\epsilon)\times(\rho/2\epsilon,\infty).

Arguing exactly as in the proof of Proposition
8.9
, we infer the existence of a constant
C^{\prime}:=C^{\prime}(M,\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}},\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor_{C^{0,\alpha}})>0
such that for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
z\in V_{\xi}

(8.31)

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0+}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{(\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon}))^{2}}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t^{\prime})-\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy\leq C^{\prime}\rho^{2\alpha}\|K^{z}\|_{L^{1}}.

For the remaining term, by performing the change of variables
s=\epsilon^{-1}t
and
s^{\prime}=\epsilon^{-1}t^{\prime}
and by using the definition of
\mathring{v}_{\epsilon}
we write for every
y\in C_{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(s^{\prime}-s)|\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(s^{\prime})-\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(s)|^{2}\,ds^{\prime}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(y+\frac{t^{\prime}}{\epsilon}\pi_{\xi}(z)+t^{\prime}\xi)-\overline{u}(y+\frac{t^{\prime}}{\epsilon}\pi_{\xi}(z)+t^{\prime}\xi)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}(y+\frac{t^{\prime}}{\epsilon}\pi_{\xi}(z)+t^{\prime}\xi)-z_{1}(0)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{\epsilon y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-z_{1}(0)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt.

By taking the integral with respect to
y
and by exploiting the
C_{\rho}(0)
-periodicity we have

\displaystyle\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon},\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon})}K^{z}(t^{\prime}-t)|\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t^{\prime})-\mathring{v}^{yz}_{\epsilon,\xi}(t)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)-\overline{u}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)-z_{1}(0)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{z}_{1}(\frac{\epsilon y}{t}+\pi_{\xi}(z))-z_{1}(0)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

From (
8.29
), we deduce that, by letting
f_{\epsilon}\colon(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})\to\mathbb{R}
be defined as

f_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}):=\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}|\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)-\overline{u}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)|^{2}\,dy,

then
f_{\epsilon}\to 0
in
L^{1}
as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
. Therefore, by Proposition
4.2
we infer that for a.e. (sufficiently small)
\rho>0
the
\epsilon_{\ell}
-traces of
f_{\epsilon_{\ell}}
relative to
(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
converge on
\{-\frac{\rho}{2}\}\cup\{\frac{\rho}{2}\}
to
0
. By using that
(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\subset(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
, from the monotonicity of the
\epsilon
-traces convergence with respect to set inclusion, we deduce also that the
\epsilon_{\ell}
-traces of
f_{\epsilon_{\ell}}
relative to
(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})
converge on
\{-\frac{\rho}{2}\}\cup\{\frac{\rho}{2}\}
to
0
, whenever
(\epsilon_{\ell})
is infinitesimal. We are thus in position to apply Proposition
4.4
and infer

(8.32)

\limsup_{\ell}\Lambda_{\ell}\left(f_{\epsilon_{\ell}}\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})};(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2}),(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})\right)=0,\ \ \text{ for a.e. $\rho>0$}

Finally, by (
8.32
), for almost every
\rho>0
we write

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell}\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon_{\ell}}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon_{\ell}}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}_{\epsilon_{\ell}}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)-\overline{u}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell}\frac{3}{\epsilon_{\ell}}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon_{\ell}}(t^{\prime}-t)|f_{\epsilon_{\ell}}\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})}(t^{\prime})-f_{\epsilon_{\ell}}\mathbbm{1}_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})}(t)|dt^{\prime}dt=0.

Hence, in view of the arbitrariness of the subsequence
(\epsilon_{\ell})
we infer

(8.33)

\limsup_{\epsilon}\int_{C_{\rho}(0)}\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\int_{(-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2})\times(-\infty,-\frac{\rho}{2})}K^{z}_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime}-t)|\overline{u}_{\epsilon}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)-\overline{u}(y+t^{\prime}\xi)|^{2}\,dt^{\prime}dt\bigg)dy=0.

With the very same technique, Propositions
4.5
and
4.4
entail that for every
\rho>0
there holds
